Vadanoorkalai refers to other arts and sciences related to the Nayanar's lineage. The texts for these are all in Sanskrit. Therefore, he mastered these as well and excelled. In addition, there is the pure art of weaponry. This is the training in warfare undertaken to protect the people of this world. In these days, this profession leads to great destruction of people, unacceptable harm, and sin and murder. When war comes, there is a practice of attacking innocent people and civilians in these days. But in ancient times, this art of weaponry, the art of war, was undertaken to protect the world. That is why the divine Sekkizhar Peruman refers to it as "Thooiya padaikkalath thozhil" (pure art of weaponry). Pure profession means performing that art of war in a pure and righteous manner. There were pure rules established in those days, such as not attacking those who turn their backs and flee, innocents, women, cows, Brahmins, the elderly, and the sick. Wars in those days followed these rules. That is why the divine Sekkizhar Peruman refers to that profession as the pure art of weaponry. In addition, Paranjothiyar not only learned elephant riding and horse riding as an art, but he also became a commander who could lead an army to victory against enemies. The fourth verse: "Ulla nirai kalaithuraigal ozhivinri payindru avatral Thelli vadithu arindha porul Sivan kazhalil serivu endre Kollum unarvinil munne kootru uthaitha kazharkku anbu Pallamadaiyaay endrum payindru varum panbudaiyar." This is the verse. Paranjothiyar continuously studied and mastered all the arts and sciences that filled his mind. These are arts related to the body and the world. In addition, he found fulfillment in his heart by realizing the soul and the Lord. Therefore, he continuously studied and mastered these arts and sciences, and through his understanding, the essence he distilled from all of them was that devotion to Lord Shiva's feet is the true discipline. Therefore, the ultimate truth, the essence that comes from examining and clarifying all the arts and sciences, is to have intense devotion to Lord Shiva's feet.

He understood this as the ultimate truth. With such clear understanding, his devotion to the feet that once kicked Yama (the god of death) flowed continuously and swiftly, like water flowing in a channel, a characteristic he always possessed. "Pallamadaiyaay" refers to the traditional practice of agriculture. The opening through which water flows from the sluice gates to the fields is called "madai." A field where water flows from a "pallamadai" is a fertile land that yields abundant crops, never lacking water. Similarly, Paranjothi Nayanar's devotion flowed continuously in his heart, words, and deeds, like water flowing from above to below. The Thiruvachakam states, "Pallam thazhuru punalil keezh melaga pathaithu urugum avar" (He melts and trembles like water flowing down a slope). Now, the fifth verse: "Eesan adiyarkku endrum iyalbana pani seidhe Aasil pugazh mannavan pal anukkaray avarkku aga Poosal munaikkaliru ukaithu por vendru porum arasar Thesangal pala kondu ther vendhan pal sirandhar." Paranjothi Nayanar always performs natural services to Shiva's devotees. This is due to the love that naturally arises in his heart, and he does whatever is necessary for Shiva's devotees. Similarly, he is a close companion to the king of flawless fame, out of love. He is an intimate companion. A king having flawless fame means ruling justly, protecting his subjects, and not harming other kings and people through unjust wars. The fame that arises from these actions is flawless fame for a king. Such a king rules excellently. Paranjothiyar is an intimate companion to such a king. This king is identified by historians as Narasimhavarman, the son of the Pallava king Mahendravarman. In the next verse, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman will describe the Vathapi war in the northern region. Therefore, the Vathapi war, also known as the Badami war, is known from inscriptions to have occurred between 640 and 643 CE. This period is also the time of this king, Narasimhavarman Pallava. Through this, the period of Paranjothiyar, Siruthonda Nayanar, is also determined. Since the history of Thirugnanasambandar Peruman's visit to Thiruchengattangudi is associated with this period, historians and researchers conclude that the period of Thirugnanasambandar Peruman is also this period. Therefore, this information is the primary and most significant aid in researching the period of the Nayanmars. They also state that the period of Mahendravarman Pallava is between 625 and 650 CE. This research was first conducted by Professor P. Sundaram Pillai. Paranjothiyar was so close to such a king, having an intimate relationship, that the king consulted him. Now, the sixth verse: "Mannavarkku thandu poy vadapulathu vathapi Thol nagaram thoolaga thulai nedungai varai ukaithu Panmaniyum nithikkuvaiyum pagattinnum parithogaiyum Innana ennilla kavarndhe ikal arasan mun konarndhar." Paranjothiyar, as an intimate companion and commander to the king, led an army and went to the northern region, where he conquered the ancient city of Vathapi, also known as Badami, by deploying an elephant army, reducing it to dust. He led the army against the enemies for the king. That city is referred to by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman as "vadapulathu vathavi thol nagaram" (the ancient city of Vathavi in the northern region). This is a city known as Badami. Before independence, Badami was located in the Belgaum district of the Bombay Presidency. Currently, this city is located in the state of Karnataka. It was the capital of the Chalukya king Pulikesi. We learn from inscriptions that Paranjothiyar, as the commander, destroyed this city of Badami in the war between the Chalukya king and the Pallava king Narasimhavarman. The remnants of the strong, ruined fort still exist there today. Not only did he conquer that king and Badami, but Paranjothiyar also brought many jewels, piles of wealth, herds of elephants, groups of horses, and countless other things and presented them before his king. These were customary practices during wars in those days. It was a tradition for victors in war to take all the possessions of their enemies. If the enemy king submitted during a war, they would receive many treasures as tribute and return. If the enemy king did not submit and fought, then conquering them and seizing their symbols was an act performed by victorious commanders. Here, since the enemy king, the Chalukya king, fought without submitting, Paranjothiyar destroyed his capital fort and brought such goods as symbols of war. There is another note here. It is said that Paranjothiyar also brought a statue of Lord Ganesha from that city as a memorial and installed that Lord Ganesha in the Ganapatheeswaram temple in his city of Thiruchengattangudi, where he is widely known and worshipped as Vathapi Ganapathi. The seventh verse: "Kathir mudi mannanum ivartham kalitru urimai aanmaiyinai Adhisayithu pugazhndhu uraippa arindha amaichargal uraippar Mathi anindhar thiruthondu vaiththa vali udaimaiyinal Ethir ivarkku ivvulagil illai ena eduthu uraithar." The king with the radiant crown marvels at and praises Paranjothiyar's prowess in leading the elephant army. The king is amazed to see that the impregnable fort of Badami was reduced to dust and that many symbols, including elephants, were captured. Therefore, he praises Paranjothiyar's valor and skill. It was customary for kings of that era to praise and honor their army commanders. The king, knowing the true reason for this victory, praised the army commander. The wise ministers who were with the king then told him the reason. They told the king the reason for Paranjothiyar's victory. They explained that because he possessed the strength of performing service to Lord Shiva, there was no one in this world who could fight against him. That was the reason. Those who possess the firm strength of service to Shiva are capable of conquering anyone in this world. It is difficult for anyone to defeat them. We have seen how the king who was allied with the wicked Jains was defeated by the strength of divine service. We have seen in Thirukoottachirappu that those who serve the feet of the universal cause are worthy of ruling the entire world. Therefore, they explained that such valor came from the firm love for Lord Shiva. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 03 — Siruthonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 926, by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. That Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarukkam, Siruthonda Nayanar Puranam. The eighth verse: "Thamberuman thiruthondar enakketta thar vendhan Umbar piran adiyarai unarade kettolindhen Vembu kodum por munaiyil vittirundhen ena veruvutru Emperuman idhu porukka vendum ena irainjanan." When the king heard that Paranjothiyar was a devotee of his Lord, a devotee who served Lord Shiva and his devotees, he trembled, saying, "I, without realizing that he was a devotee of the Lord, sent him to the fierce battlefield to fight!" The ministers had only told the king that the reason for Paranjothiyar's victory was his devotion to Shiva. But upon hearing this, the king, who had great love for Lord Shiva, abandoned the thought of victory and trembled, realizing his transgression, "I sent him to the battlefield without knowing for so long that he was a great devotee!" This shows us the depth of the king's great love for Lord Shiva and his devotees. Thus, the king, fearing, said to Paranjothiyar, "My Lord, please forgive this mistake," and bowed down. Forgetting his position as a commander serving under him, the king, upon learning that Paranjothiyar possessed the strength of divine service, addressed him as "Emperuman" (My Lord) and bowed, revealing the depth of his mature devotion to Shiva. His plea to Paranjothiyar to forgive his mistake was also for this reason. The ninth verse: "Irainjathalum mun irainji en urimaithozhilkku adutha Thiram puriven adarkku enno theengu enna aangavarkku Niraindha nithikkuvaigaludan needu viruthigal aliththe Aram puri sengol arasan anjali seidhu uraikkindran." This is the ninth verse, and following it, the tenth verse: "Ummudaiya nilaimaiyinai ariyamaal kondu uyndheer Emmodaiya manakkaruthukku inithay isaindhum umadhu Meimmai puri seyal vilanga vendiyavare sarithu Semmai neri thiruthondu seyyum ena vidai koduthan." These two verses follow. We saw that the king bowed down to Paranjothiyar. When he bowed down, Paranjothiyar replied to the king, "I only performed my rightful duty of warfare, taking on the leadership of the army and serving as an intimate companion to the king. I was not commanded by you to do any inappropriate or wrong act. Therefore, no harm will come to you," Paranjothiyar told the king. At that very moment, the king gave Paranjothiyar countless piles of wealth and long-lasting sarvamanyams (tax-free grants of land), and the righteous king, who ruled according to dharma, stood with folded hands and bowed to Paranjothiyar. This age is a young age for Paranjothiyar, not yet mature. He possessed excellent training in weaponry and unparalleled leadership skills as a commander. He had won many battles, conquered many enemy kingdoms, and enriched the government. He had reduced the difficult-to-conquer ancient city of Badami to dust, defeated the enemy, and brought fame and glory to the king. If he had continued to serve the king for many more days, the kingdom would have flourished even more. Even though there were many more years for him to serve the king and then retire from that service, the king, knowing all this, bowed to Paranjothiyar, realizing that his service to Shiva was far greater than his service as a commander, and that it was greater than any worldly profession. The king requested him to continue his service to Shiva, granting him countless piles of wealth and grants. From now on, Paranjothiyar should not engage in any other profession for his livelihood or serve anyone. The king gave him all the sarvamanyams, lands, and piles of wealth so that he could live without worry, serving only Lord Shiva. He gave him piles of wealth. He also gave him lands so that it would not be spent away easily. He gave them as tax-free lands. This is because he should help the Shiva devotee to perform his Shiva devotion, not keep him in service and command him. The king understood that it was a great sin to do so. Therefore, he acted accordingly. The Shiva dharma rules, as stated in the Vedas and Shivagamas, are superior to worldly dharma. Thus, the king gave all these and bowed with folded hands. He bowed to Paranjothiyar. He further said, "I led you without knowing your true state of devotion. Now, you must agree to my heartfelt desire. May your true acts of devotion shine brightly in the world as you wish, and perform your service to Shiva, the path of righteousness." With these words, he released him from his duty in the army, which was under him, and freed Paranjothiyar from that obligation. He requested him to perform only Shiva devotion. This was the king's decision and judgment. Paranjothiyar had said that he had only performed his rightful duty and that there was no harm in it, but the king did not accept it. Worldly duties are different. The nature of Shiva's servitude is different. The latter is far superior and greater than the former. Politics is concerned with the welfare of the people's bodies and ends there. The nature of Shiva, which is superior, benefits the soul. Thus, the king granted him release from royal service. Now, the 11th, 12th, and 13th verses: "Mannavanai vidai kondu thampathiyil vandhadaindhu Pannu pugazh Paranjothiyar thamum panimathi vazh Senniyarai Ganapathichcharaththu irainji thiruthondu Munna nilaiyin vazhuva murai anbil seykkindrar. Vedakkarunar adiyar vendiya meippani seyya Theethil kudi pirandha Thiruvenkattu Nangai enum Kadhal manaikkizhaththiyar karuththu ondra varum perumai Neethi manai aram puriyum nermaiyinai nilai nirpar. Narai ithazhi thirumudiyar adiyarai nal thorum Muraimaiyinil thiru amudhu mun ootti pin unnum Niraiyudaiya peruviruppal niyathiyaga kollum Thurai vazhuva vagai ozhugum thooya thozhil thalai nindrar." These three verses describe Paranjothiyar taking leave of the king and returning to his native place, Thiruchengattangudi. Historians say that the king's city was Kanchipuram. From there, Paranjothiyar set out and reached his sacred place, Thiruchengattangudi, which is near Thiruvarur in the Chola country. Paranjothiyar worships the Lord, who wears the moon on his head, at Ganapatheechcharam. One can worship the Lord anywhere and everywhere, in all things. He is brilliantly and clearly enshrined in the Ganapatheechcharam temple. Paranjothiyar worships him in the Ganapatheechcharam temple. Thiruchengattangudi is the name of the sacred place. Ganapatheechcharam is the name of the temple. When Vinayakamurthy killed Gajamukasuran, the place was named Sengattangudi because of the overflowing blood. After that, Ganapathi installed and worshipped a Shiva lingam. He performed Shiva puja. Therefore, the temple came to be known as Ganapatheechcharam.

We can see this in detail in the Gayamukan Uthpatti Padalam of the Kandapuranam. Due to his great penance in the past, Paranjothiyar performs divine service in Thiruchengattangudi without fail, according to the rules prescribed by the Shiva Agamas, with love. While performing all the true services required by the devotees of Lord Shiva, who is the cause of the Vedas, he and his wife, named Thiruvenkattu Nangai, together perform Shiva dharmas with a unified mind. Paranjothiyar marries the lady named Thiruvenkattu Nangai. Our Nayanar, who performs Shiva devotion with proper love, undertook this householder's dharma solely to serve the devotees. The Thirukkural states, "Thenpulathar deivam virundhu okkal than" (The ancestors, God, guests, and relatives). It also states, "Thurandharkkum thuvvathavarkkum endrum" (For ascetics and the destitute). The Thirukkural also states, "Ilvalvan enban iyalbudaiya moovarkkum endrum" (The householder is for the three natural ones). Thus, the book of ethics outlines the duties of those who engage in householder's life. But all those are worldly dharmas. The divine service to Shiva's devotees mentioned here is of a different nature altogether. All worldly dharmas turn into corresponding merits, give corresponding enjoyments, and lead to rebirth. But the divine service to devotees, being different and superior to worldly dharmas, becomes the very service to Shiva and ultimately leads to liberation. Here, our Nayanar did not marry for worldly pleasures or for progeny. He married solely to perform true service to devotees. We can confirm this in the events that follow in this sacred story. We can also recall the sacred stories of great individuals like Ilaiyankudi Mara Nayanar, Iyarpagai Nayanar, Kungiliyakkalaiya Nayanar, who served devotees while remaining in the householder's state. We see in their respective stories that they too lived a householder's life with the sole aim of serving devotees. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman mentions the name of Paranjothiyar's divine wife as Thiruvenkattu Nangai. Her birth name is not known to us. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman mentions the lady's name as Thiruvenkattu Nangai, referring to the town of Thiruvenkadu. Just as Karaikkal Ammaiyar is known, this lady's name is widely known as Thiruvenkattu Nangai. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman mentions that the lineage she was born into is a "theethil kudi" (flawless lineage). It is a good lineage where both families are pure. We can recall the Thirumurugatrupadai, which speaks of "iru marabum thoolku thol kudi" (ancient lineage of two pure traditions). The householder's life of Paranjothiyar and Thiruvenkattu Nangai Ammaiyar was also filled with love, as described in Akapporul texts. As Avvaiyar said, "Kadhal iruvar karuththu orumiththu adharavu pattathe inbam" (Love is the joy when two minds unite and support each other), their minds were united. In what were they united? Their minds were united in performing divine service to devotees and for Shiva's puja. We will see this greatness throughout this sacred story. Thus, engaging in the householder's dharma, Paranjothiyar daily followed the rule of first offering sacred food to the devotees of Lord Shiva, who wears a garland of honey-laden Konrai flowers on his head, according to scriptural injunctions, and only then partaking of it himself. He excelled in such pure service and devotion. Therefore, in these three verses, we see the appropriateness of first mentioning Paranjothiyar's service to Shiva, then stating that he undertook the householder's dharma for the sake of serving devotees, and then describing his continued service to devotees while remaining in that householder's dharma. Now, the divine Sekkizhar Pillaiyar Peruman will explain how he offered sacred food to the devotees. Let us continue to contemplate that. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 04 — Siruthonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 927, by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman, Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarukkam, Siruthonda Nayanar Puranam. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman sings about how Siruthonda Nayanar offered sacred food to the devotees. The 14th verse: "Thooya thiru amudhu kani kannal arusuvai kari ney Paaya thayir pal inniya panniyam unnir amudham Meya padiyal amudhu seivikka isaindhu adiyar Mayiru nyalam potra varum ivarpal manam magizhndhar." This verse describes how sacred food should be prepared for devotees. Paranjothiyar was an excellent commander and a great devotee of the king. Therefore, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman elaborates that he prepared all these without any deficiency and offered them to the devotees. Previously, in the Ilaiyankudi Nayanar Puranam, he generally mentioned "undi nalu vidathil aru suvai thiraththinil" (four kinds of food with six tastes). Here, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman elaborates on this point. He also explains this to us in a way that emphasizes and teaches us. He offered pure sacred food, fruits, jaggery, six kinds of curries, ghee, thick curd, milk, sweet delicacies, and nectar-like drinking water to the devotees in an appropriate manner. "Thiru amudhu" here refers to cooked rice made from red paddy. "Pal" refers to milk that has been thickened by boiling. "Unnir amudham" here refers to purified water for drinking. Drinking water for thirst is different. Purified water, which is an important part of the meal when eating, is different. "Paaya thayir" refers to curd that has been set and is thick. All these were done regularly every day. Thus, when sacred food was served, all the devotees were greatly pleased and rejoiced. The 15th verse: "Seetha mathi aravinudan senjadai mel seriviththa Nathan adiyar thammai nayappattu vazhipattal Methakaiyar avar munbu migach chiriyarai adaindhar Adhalinal Siruthondar ena nigazhndhar avaniyin mel." We saw that the Nayanar's birth name was Paranjothiyar. The name Siruthondar is prevalent in the world for him. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman sings in this verse the reason for that name. He worships the devotees of Lord Shiva, who wears the cool moon and snakes on his red matted hair, with devotion. He considered himself very small in the presence of the great Shiva devotees and served at their feet. Therefore, he became known by the name Siruthondar (the small devotee) in the world. Although Paranjothiyar was the Nayanar's birth name and was praised by many, the name Siruthondar became very famous and prevalent in the world. It will be praised by Sundaramurthy Swamigal in the Thiruthondathogai Thiruppathigam. This name also seems to be in accordance with the Nayanar's wish. That is why the name Siruthondar became more widely praised and used than his birth name Paranjothiyar. He saw himself as very small in the presence of the greatness of the devotees and served them. Whenever he saw devotees, he thought, "Where is their greatness? Where is my smallness? By their grace, they have come to take me in, without considering their greatness." And he acted accordingly. That is the nature of Paranjothi Nayanar. Just as Thirunavukkarasar Peruman's Thevaram says, "Ethanaiyum ariya nee eliyai aanay eliyenai vandhu erangi endru kondai Ethanaiyum emperamo aiya ayyo emperuman thirukkarunai irundhavare" (You, who are so rare, became simple; you came and embraced me, the lowly one. Is all this mine, oh Lord? Alas, alas, how great is the divine grace of our Lord!), Siruthonda Nayanar's nature is similar. Now, the 16th and 17th verses: "Kannuthalar Ganapathichcharaththinkan karuththu amara Ullanirai anbinil pani seidhu ozhuguvar vazhuvinri Ennil perum seer adiyar idaividadhu amudhu seyya Nanniya per uvakaiyudan nayandhu uraiyum nalin kan Neerarum sadaimudiyar arulinal nirai thavaththu Peralar avarthamakku perugu thirumanai araththin Veragi vilangum Thiruvenkattu Nangai pal Seeralathevar enum thirumaindhar avatharithar." Siruthonda Nayanar serves with unwavering devotion and overflowing love in his heart, focusing his thoughts on Lord Shiva, who has an eye on his forehead, at Ganapatheechcharam. While countless great devotees continuously partook of the sacred food, Siruthonda Nayanar, filled with great joy, resided there with pleasure. By the grace of Lord Shiva, who has water in his matted hair, a divine son named Seeraladevar was born from the womb of Thiruvenkattu Nangai, who was the root of the flourishing householder's dharma for Siruthonda Nayanar, the great ascetic whose penance was complete. The birth of the divine son was due to Shiva's grace, which flowed from Siruthonda Nayanar's service to Shiva and his feeding of the devotees. Siruthonda Nayanar's penance was complete, and his penance was to be fulfilled by this progeny. Such a great ascetic, whose penance was complete, had a divine son born to him through his wife, Thiruvenkattu Nangai Ammaiyar. The wife, the householder, is the root of the householder's dharma, which is a form of penance. We will see the greatness of their householder's life in the events that follow in this sacred story. To such a Siruthonda Nayanar, a divine son named Seeraladevar was born from the womb of his divine wife. How many great qualities does Seeraladevar possess! Seeraladevar is the source of the events in the Siruthonda Nayanar Puranam. Seeraladevar is the one who received the great blessing of being called the Lord's desired offering. Seeralan Siruthondan is the one who received the blessing of being mentioned in the divine words of Thirugnanasambandar Peruman. He is the one who received the unique blessing of being taken by the Lord, along with Lord Muruga and the Goddess. He is destined to receive great blessings. He possesses so many great qualities, and therefore he was praised as "Needu Maganar" (the long-lived son) and "Seeraladevar enum Thirumaindhar" (the divine son named Seeraladevar) by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. The 18th and 19th verses: "Arumaiyinil thani puthalvar pirandha pozhudhu alangariththa Perumaiyinil kilai kalippa pirarkkariya mani pettru varum Magizhchi thathaiyar manaththu adangaa vagai valara Thiru mali neyyadal vizha Sengattangudi eduppa Mangala nalliyam muzhakka marai muzhakkam van alappa Anganar tham seer adiyarkku alavirandha nithi aliththu Thangal marabinil urimai sadangu dasa thinaththinilum Pongu perum magizhchiyudan purindhu kaappu ani punaindhar." These two verses describe the joyful events and auspicious ceremonies that took place during the divine birth of Seeraladevar. When the rare and unique son was born, decorations were made, and the relatives rejoiced in that greatness. They performed decorations, including household decorations. The father rejoiced at having received a son who was difficult to obtain. It was customary in those days to understand and know the countless great qualities of the divine son through astrology, the child's physical structure, and other grammatical texts at the time of birth. Therefore, the father was very happy that he had received a son who was rare to others. The joyful ceremony that all outsiders perform for a good childbirth is the Neyyadal Vizha (ghee bathing ceremony). We see this in the divine birth of Aludaiya Pillaiyar Thirugnanasambandar Peruman. Some also call it Ennai Adal Vizha (oil bathing ceremony). Now, auspicious musical instruments are played. Vedic mantras are chanted to bless. Siruthonda Nayanar gives immeasurable wealth to the Lord's great devotees. He performs the customary rituals of his lineage for 10 days. There is also a tradition of performing rituals for 10 days after birth. There are also lineages that perform rituals for 16 days. These vary from lineage to lineage. They adorn the child with the sacred ash as a protective charm. The 20th verse: "Arvam nirai perum sutram akamalar aliththu avartham Par perugum magizhchiyudan paruvamurai parattu Seer peruga seyya valar thiru maganar seeradaiyil Thar valar kinkini asaiya thalar nadaiyin patham sarnthan." After the child's birth, giving special gifts to relatives with great affection and rejoicing is also one of the rituals. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman first mentions the greatness of giving wealth to Shiva's devotees and then states that Siruthonda Nayanar also gives great wealth to his relatives with a joyful heart. In those days, Siruthonda Nayanar performed all the seasonal rituals with excellence. The growing divine son, Seeraladevanayanar, reached the stage of toddling, with the jingling anklets, strung like a garland, swaying on his small divine feet, as described in the Pillaitamil stages, starting from the Sengirrai Paruvam. We have contemplated these stages and their special rituals in detail in the Thirugnanasambandar Peruman Puranam. Thus, as these seasonal rituals were performed, Siruthonda Nayanar's divine son, Seeraladevanayanar, grew and reached the stage of toddling, with the jingling anklets, strung like a garland, swaying on his small divine feet. The 21st verse: "Surulum mayir nuthal sutti thunaikkathin manik kudambai Maruvu thirukkanda nan marbinil aimpadai kaiyil Poruvil vairach sarigal pon araignan punai sadhangai Theruvil oli vilanga valar thiru vilaiyattinil amardhar." In this verse, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman shows us the divine form of Seeraladevanayanar, adorned in his childhood, from head to toe. He wears a forehead ornament on his curly hair-filled forehead. Curly hair falling on the forehead is a sign of physical beauty. He wears diamond earrings in both ears. Kudambai is an earring worn for the growth of the ears. It is made of precious stones. He wears a golden chain called "nan" around his sacred neck. He wears an "aimpadai thali" on his chest. The "aimpadai thali" is an ornament made of gold, depicting the five weapons of Vishnu – conch, discus, sword, mace, and bow – which are believed to protect the child as it grows. It is customary for this to protect the child. He wears bracelets on his divine hands and armlets on his divine shoulders. He wears a golden waist-cord around his waist. He wears anklets on his divine feet. Thus, adorned with all these ornaments, from head to toe, from the forehead ornament to the anklets, Seeraladevar, shining brightly in the street, delights in his divine play. The 22nd verse: "Vandhu valar moovandil mayir vinai mangalam seidhu Thanthaiyarum payandha thayarum thanich chiruvar Sinthai malar sol thelivil sezhungalaigal payila tham Banthamara vandhavarai palliyinil iruththinar." Seeraladeva Nayanar is growing well. He reaches his third year. In the third year, they perform the auspicious ritual of hair removal, which is called Saula Kalyanam. The father and the mother, who gave birth to him, placed their unique little son in school so that he could learn the rich arts with clarity of words, which would make his mind blossom, and so that their birth bond would be severed. Seeraladevar did not come there for himself. He did not take birth for himself. He was born to sever the birth bond of his parents, which was due to their attachment. We will see in this historical event how he became the means to sever the birth of his parents by severing the worldly attachment of a son. Therefore, they placed him in school, the one who came to sever their bond. The 23rd verse: "Annallil Sanpai nagar andagaiyar ezhundharula Munnaga ethirkondu kodu pugundhu munnool ser Pon marbil Siruthondar pugali kavalartham Nannama sevadihal potri isaiththu nalam sirandhar." In those days, when Aludaiya Pillaiyar Thirugnanasambandar Peruman, the Lord of Sirkazhi, came to Thiruchengattangudi, Siruthondar, whose chest was adorned with the sacred thread, went forward to receive him, brought him into the city, and made him reside in his house. He praised the good and glorious feet of Thirugnanasambandar Peruman, the protector of Pugali, and attained great welfare. He welcomed Thirugnanasambandar Peruman into his divine house and honored him. The 24th verse: "Sanpaiyartham perumanum thangiya perungathal Panbudaiya Siruthondarudan payindru matru avai Man paravum thiruppathigam thanil vaiththu sirappiththu Nanbaruli ezhundharula tham inithu nayapputrar." We also saw in the Puranam of Thirugnanasambandar Peruman that Thirugnanasambandar Peruman came to Thiruchengattangudi and resided in Siruthonda Nayanar's divine house. Aludaiya Pillaiyar, the Lord of Sirkazhi, resided happily with Siruthondar, who possessed the great and ever-growing love that was difficult to bear. In accordance with "Anbarodum maree" (mingling with devotees), Thirugnanasambandar Peruman praised Siruthonda Nayanar's greatness in a Thiruppathigam that would be praised by the world and honored him. Only those who are born in this world with the blessing of merit and follow the path of Shiva can spread and attain salvation through the Thiruppathigams of Thirugnanasambandar Peruman. Even other celestial beings and others are unable to understand and spread them. In such a Thiruppathigam of Thirugnanasambandar Peruman, he praised Siruthonda Nayanar. We recall here the great blessings that came from Pillaiyar's divine words, where even inanimate palm trees attained liberation and a bone became a woman. We see that Thirugnanasambandar Peruman praised Siruthonda Nayanar in the Thiruppathigam for Thiruchengattangudi, saying, "Podhi nugarum Siruthondarkku arul seyyum poruttaga Kadinagaray veetrirundhan Ganapathichcharane" (For the sake of bestowing grace upon Siruthondar who partakes of the sacred ash, Ganapathichcharan resided in the strong city) and "Seeralan Siruthondan Sengattangudi meya Peralan" (Seeralan, Siruthondan, the great one who resides in Sengattangudi). In addition to praising him, Thirugnanasambandar Peruman bestowed upon Siruthonda Nayanar the great blessing of being his friend. Nambiyandar Nambigal Swamigal also sang about this in the Aludaiya Pillaiyar Thiru Andhadhi. The Thirukkural states, "Unarchithan natpam kizhamai tharum" (Understanding brings friendship and kinship). It was the great compassion of our Paramacharyar, Thirugnanasambandar Peruman, that he bestowed the blessing of friendship upon Siruthondar, who served at his divine feet. Thus, bestowing the blessing of friendship, Thirugnanasambandar Peruman departed from Thiruchengattangudi to other sacred places. Siruthonda Nayanar and his wife always contemplated Pillaiyar's greatness and rejoiced. In this sacred story of Siruthonda Nayanar, the Lord will now come as Bhairava and take Siruthonda Nayanar and Seeraladevar into his grace. We rejoice to see how the greatness of Seeraladevar, which will be revealed in the historical event of the great grace that the Lord will now bestow upon them, was previously praised by Thirugnanasambandar Peruman in these Thiruppathigams as a premonition by divine grace. Now, we will continue to contemplate the very important section where the Lord comes as Bhairava. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 05 — Siruthonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 928, authored by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarukkam. Siruthonda Nayanar Puranam. Lord Shiva, leaving Mount Kailash, assumes the form of Bhairava to experience the devotion of Siruthonda Nayanar and bestow His grace upon him. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman, in 11 verses, describes the Lord's departure from Kailash and the Bhairava form He took. Verses 25 to 35 are as follows: On a day when such events were unfolding, Siruthondar's service reached the sacred feet of the Lord of Kailash. To partake of his true and unwavering devotion, the Lord, in the form of Bhairava, descended from the sacred mountain. His long matted locks, adorned with Konrai flowers, were like the dark clouds that rise from the ocean, swirling, dark, dense, disheveled, swaying, and thickly spread like a cluster of black hair, resembling a beautiful, well-groomed coiffure. Amidst this dark mass of hair, like stars scattered in the sky, were the fragrant Thumbai flowers, buzzing with swarms of black, honey, and iridescent bees. Near His sacred head, a crescent moon, as if transformed into a full moon, was placed, resembling a single, radiant white ash mark on His forehead, which glowed like the fiery red coral spreading its brilliant light. His sacred ears, adorned with conch earrings, within which red Arattai flowers were placed, shone brightly, as if the sun, moon, and fire had come together, captivating all beauty. To conceal the poison in His throat, a pure white garland of pearl-like beads, resembling a string of ambrosial bubbles from the ocean, adorned His neck, causing the hearts and souls of those who contemplated Him to melt and overflow. His dark skin, like the evening light of the crimson sun setting in the ocean, was covered by the black elephant hide, worn as a garment, as if the darkness of night enveloped the red glow. His body, radiant with the love of His devotees, was adorned with rosaries of rudraksha beads, necklaces, armlets, waistbands, and anklets, all shining with a unique brilliance. Seeing the immense grace with which the Lord came to bestow blessings upon His unparalleled devotee on earth, may you cherish the Lord's grace and follow the path of love, as if the Vedas themselves, in the form of anklets on His sacred feet, resonated throughout all directions. His left hand held a Brahma Kapala, while the radiant, three-pronged trident rested on His sacred shoulder. His right hand, shining brightly, held a Damaru, whose sound resonated. The earth, having performed great penance, bore His lotus feet, receiving His grace. A gentle smile, like moonlight, illuminated His gracious face, and the sharp trident, dispelling the three impurities, shone brightly. As overflowing love, rich in meaning, surged forth, the Lord arrived in Thiruchengattangudi, a fertile land in the Tamil country, renowned for its clear and abundant Tamil, to be praised by the world. These are the verses. Siruthonda Nayanar's devotion reached the sacred feet of the Lord residing on the great Mount Kailash. It was deeply intertwined with the all-pervading fullness of the Lord's feet. Siruthonda Nayanar's true devotion, unwavering and constant like the nature of the Lord Himself, was cherished by the Lord, who desired to reside within it. To experience and bless this true devotion of Siruthonda Nayanar, the Lord, in a joyful Bhairava form, descended from Mount Kailash, the sacred mountain. The description here is of the Lord, having assumed the Bhairava form, arriving in Thiruchengattangudi in the guise of a Bhairava Jangama. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman, seeing the nature and splendor of the Lord's form, from head to foot, describes it to us so that we too may worship and attain salvation. The form the Lord took was that of a Bhairava Jangama. Bhairavaism is a sub-sect within Saivism, considered one of the inner sects of Saivism. Mahavrata, Kapala, Bhairava, etc., are sub-divisions of Saivism. Among these, the Lord arrives in the form of a Jangama belonging to the Bhairava sect. The fact that he is referred to simply as Bhairava, rather than Bhairava Jangama, stems from the tradition of addressing a devotee as the Lord Himself. How does that form appear? The Lord transforms His own reddish, long matted locks into a mass of dark clouds, spreading them out as a thick, long, black mane. Up to the first verse, all the Lord's forms appear in His true divine form. However, He adorns Himself with various objects, but transforms them to suit the Bhairava Jangama form, so that ordinary people do not recognize Him. The Lord's long matted locks, adorned with Konrai flowers, are spread out beautifully like a cluster of black hair, resembling the swirling, continuous, dark, dense, disheveled, swaying, and thickly growing hair of a black cloud rising from the dark ocean. He spreads His long matted locks, which are long and curly, making them into a black mane. "Kunchi" refers to a man's hair. "Pangi" refers to curly hair. It is curly, dark, dense like a forest, disheveled like the waves of black sand, swaying, and thick. All these indicate the curly nature of His sacred hair. Following the tradition of describing from head to foot, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman first describes the hair. Amidst this cloud-like black hair, which is as beautiful as if made entirely of black collyrium, the Thumbai flowers on the Lord's sacred head shine like stars appearing in the sky. His sacred head is like a cloud, and the Thumbai flowers shine like stars appearing in the gaps of that cloud. Swarms of black, honey, and iridescent bees buzz around these Thumbai flowers. The fragrant Thumbai flower is one of the flowers dear to the Lord. The Lord, who wears the crescent moon on His sacred head, like the young moon seen at dusk, appears with a single, radiant white ash mark on His forehead, as if He has transformed that moon into a full moon and placed it there. The Lord's sacred forehead is a radiant forehead that emits the rays of a brilliant, fiery red coral light. On it, He wears a single white ash mark. It is like the crescent moon of Akhileswara, transformed into a full moon, worn as a single white ash mark on His forehead. Those belonging to the Bhairava sect do not smear their entire forehead with white ash, but rather wear a large, single mark. The Lord also appears in the same form here. As if the fiery sun, the radiant moon, and the fire element had come together, His sacred ears, which captivate beauty, are adorned with beautiful conch earrings, within which red Arattai flowers are placed, shining brightly on both sides. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman describes it not as if the sun, moon, and fire came, but as if those three spheres came together. They are placed in His sacred ears, as if adorned with conch earrings, and within those conch earrings, red Arattai flowers are also placed. The sun, moon, and fire are three things that can never be together, but the divine Sekkizhar Peruman shows us that they are together here. This is called an "aputa uvamaittiram" (an impossible simile). He has placed and adorned the red Arattai flower within the conch earring, making it shine beautifully. To conceal the poison in His sacred throat, the Lord wears a pure white garland of pearl-like beads, resembling a string of ambrosial bubbles from the ocean, around His neck, which shines like a white crystal garland. It is arranged in such a way that the bodies and souls of those who contemplate it melt. His sacred throat is the Thiruneelakantham (blue throat). To conceal it from view, the Lord wears this crystal garland around His sacred throat. The blue color on the neck, Thiruneelakantham, is a special attribute of Lord Shiva. If He were to appear with Thiruneelakantham on His neck, it would be known that He is the Lord. Therefore, to prevent His true nature from being known, He must conceal His neck. Hence, He wears a crystal garland. The large crystal bead garland around His neck, concealing the poison, shines like a string of ambrosial bubbles. Here we see the subtlety that ambrosia alone can conceal poison. As the red sun, when it dips into the ocean, gives off a reddish glow, and as the darkness of night envelops that red glow, so too the Lord, to conceal His crimson body, wears the elephant hide, which He had previously used to slay the elephant demon, as a garment, making it appear as a beautiful shirt. The elephant hide shines as a garment on the Lord's coral-like body. When the sun sets in the evening, it glows red. As it dips into the ocean, that crimson light is enveloped by the darkness of night. Similarly, the Lord appears with His old elephant hide as a garment over His crimson body. Furthermore, He appears with a shining light, adorned with garlands of bone beads, necklaces, armlets, waistbands, and anklets. The overflowing love of His devotees manifests as these ornaments on His body. The love of the devotees shines as ornaments on the Lord's body in various places. Since all the sacred ornaments are made of white bone beads, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman compares them to love. Pure white is the color associated with love. Just as Thiruneelakantha Nayanar had deep love for the Lord's neck, so too each devotee, with deep love for each of the Lord's limbs, beautifies them. Therefore, the Lord appears as if His body is the manifestation of the love of His devotees. The Sannaveeram (chain of victory), Haram (necklace), Vadam (garland), Kaichari (bracelet), Kadisutrachari (waistband), Karchari (anklet) adorn the Lord's neck, chest, arms, waist, and feet respectively, appearing as if they are the manifestation of the overflowing love of His devotees. Sannaveeram is a garland of victory, also called a chain of valor. Sannaveeram, Haram, and Vadam are worn on the chest. Vadam is a thick, hanging garland made of rudraksha beads. Haram is a long, single pearl necklace. Kaichari is a bracelet that moves on the hand. Kadisutram is a waistband. Kaivalaichari refers to bangles and other ornaments. Now, the sacred anklets on the Lord's feet resonate, their sound reaching all directions. How do they resonate? They resonate as if the Vedas themselves are proclaiming to the people of the world: "O people of the world! Behold the immense grace with which the Lord has come to bestow blessings upon His unparalleled devotee, Siruthondar, on this earth. Cherish the Lord's grace and follow the path of love!" The sacred anklets on the Lord's feet resonate, their sound reaching all directions, just as the Vedas proclaim to the people to abide in the Lord's love. This is also the inner meaning of the Vedic teachings. Now, with His left hand holding the Brahma Kapala, the Lord holds the trident on His sacred shoulder, making it shine. In His right hand, which emits light, He holds the Damaru, which resonates loudly. As the "Unmai Vilakkam" states, "Thotram Thudiyathaninil" (Creation from the Damaru), the sound of the Damaru is the cause for the re-creation and flourishing of the world that had dissolved. Damaru is a type of drum, also called Thudi, a two-headed drum played with a single striker. As the Lord's sacred hands shine in this manner, the earth, having received the fruit of its past penance, bears the Lord's lotus feet. The idea that "the earth rejoiced upon touching the Lord" from the Thiruvilaiyadal Puranam, where the Lord carried earth, comes to mind here. With such a divine form, the Lord appears with a gentle smile, like moonlight, on His gracious face, and the sharp trident, dispelling the three impurities, shines brightly, revealing the true meaning of overflowing love, so that the world may praise Him. He arrives in Thiruchengattangudi, a fertile land in the Tamil country, renowned for its clear and abundant Tamil, to be praised by the world. The moonlight of a gentle smile illuminates the Lord's sacred face, meaning that the appearance of His sacred teeth is slightly revealed by that gentle smile. The trident in the Lord's hand, which destroys the three impurities of living beings, shines with the light of the sun. The three impurities are those that cause great delusion. "Marul" (delusion) is the state of perceiving that which is not real as real, as stated in the Thirukkural. Karma Mala (impurity of karma) arises from the path of destiny. When the desire for it ceases, liberation comes. Therefore, Karma Mala must be destroyed. Maya Mala (impurity of illusion), if given by the Lord for the sake of light, like a lamp at night, must also be destroyed. Furthermore, the Anava Mala (impurity of ego), the root impurity that resides with the soul's knowledge and conceals it in the state of Kevala (solitude) and later deludes it in the state of Sakala (activity), must be destroyed, otherwise the soul will not attain liberation. Therefore, the Lord's trident, the weapon that destroys the three impurities, shines brightly. The meaning is that the trident is what the Lord holds to destroy the three impurities of living beings. The trident is Lord Shiva's weapon. It is also called Pasupatam. All the states described in the Astra Mantra within Shiva mantras can be thoroughly examined here. With such a divine form, the Lord arrives in Thiruchengattangudi, a fertile land in the Tamil country, renowned for its clear and abundant Tamil, so that the true meaning of overflowing love may flourish further and further, and the world may praise Him, bringing enlightenment. As a result of this historical event, the world will see the benefit of love and strive to attain the true meaning. The ignorant people who cling to things like family and wealth and fall into this birth will have their three impurities destroyed. Thus, they will experience the flourishing of overflowing love, rich in meaning. All these are the benefits that accrue to those who listen to this history. Therefore, for those who listen to this history, the three impurities that hinder liberation will be removed. Thus, the Lord arrives in Thiruchengattangudi, a Tamil country, to bestow that great benefit, so that the people of this world may attain the true meaning and experience the flourishing of overflowing love. That Tamil, with its generous nature, is a Tamil that bestows great knowledge. It is a Tamil capable of explaining the knowledge of Shiva's servitude. The Lord, in this Bhairava form, is about to say that He has come from Uttarapathi, the northern country. Therefore, the Lord comes from the northern country to Thiruchengattangudi in the southern Tamil country. The flourishing of love and consequently of Shivattva (Shiva-nature) is a special characteristic of the Tamil country. Thus, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman shows us the Bhairava form the Lord took. In summary, the Lord, having assumed the Bhairava form, transformed His reddish, long matted locks into a dense mass of black hair, like a cloud, and spread it out. The Thumbai flowers placed amidst this spread hair resembled stars shining amidst the dark clouds. The crescent moon tucked into His hair, as if transformed into a small full moon, appeared as a single white ash mark on His forehead. The three spheres of the sun, moon, and fire appeared as His two ears, the conch earrings, and the red Arattai flowers placed within them. To conceal the severity of the poisonous Thiruneelakantham, a garland of white crystal beads, resembling a string of ambrosial bubbles, adorned His neck. The black garment covering His body was like the darkness of night enveloping the crimson glow of His body at dusk. The old elephant hide appeared like a covering. The Sannaveeram, Haram, Vadam, Kaichari, Araichari, and Karchari appeared as if the overflowing love of His devotees had manifested in those places on His body. The sound of the anklets on His sacred feet, like the hidden anklets, seemed to proclaim to the people of the world to cherish the Lord's grace and follow the path of love, having witnessed the immense grace with which the Lord came to bestow blessings upon His devotee on earth. The trident, held by His left hand and resting on His shoulder, shone brightly, and the sound of the Damaru in His right hand resonated. His lotus feet rested on the earth. A gentle smile, like moonlight, illuminated His face. The trident shone with the light of the sun. Seeing this, the Lord, in this Bhairava form, arrived in Thiruchengattangudi in the Tamil country, to be praised by the world. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman, by showing this divine form, allows us to see and worship it from head to foot, and thus attain salvation. This divine form is one that the Lord assumed by His own will. The bodies of other deities and living beings like humans are products of Maya, created by illusion. The Lord's body is not like that. The Thiruvilaiyadal Puranam states that the various forms the Lord takes are also of this nature. Thus, the Lord assumes a divine form to manifest and bestow grace upon His great devotees. Nevertheless, due to the merits of their penance, other people living in those times also get to see the Lord. However, when His divine form appears to the outer eyes, the unique characteristic of this divine form of the Lord is that He does not come in His true nature, but only after that form has disappeared, it is known that it was the Lord Himself. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 06 — Siruthonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 929, authored by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarukkam. Siruthonda Nayanar Puranam. We saw that the Lord, in the form of Bhairava Jangama, arrived in Thiruchengattangudi. On that day, Siruthonda Nayanar had gone out of his house to find and invite a suitable Shiva devotee for the Maheswara Puja. At that time, our Lord, in the form of Bhairava Jangama, arrived in Thiruchengattangudi. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman continues with six verses, 36, 37, 38, 39, 40, 41. They are as follows: Like one with an insatiable hunger, He quickly approached Siruthondar's house, asking those He met, "Is the devotee who always offers food to the Lord's servants, the one adorned with garlands of buzzing flowers, in this house?" The maidservant, named Sandanam, a woman of fragrant beauty, came forward, bowed low, and replied, "He is a great ascetic." She said, "Our Lord, who owns us, please enter the house." The Lord looked at the young woman's face and said, "I will enter alone where the women are." Hearing this, she hesitated as if she would leave. Frightened, she quickly went to the doorstep of Thiruvengattu Ammai, Siruthondar's wife, and said, "The devotee of Ambalavar, who serves food to the Lord's servants, has not found anyone today and has gone searching. If he sees your unique and beautiful form, he will be overjoyed, considering it a great blessing. He will not delay now. He will come immediately. Please remain here." The Lord replied, "We are from Uttarapathi, those who uphold unparalleled domestic virtue. We have come to see the incomparable Siruthondar. We will not remain here if he is not present, under any circumstances." Then, the Lord, who does not reveal His third eye, said, "We are staying under the beautiful flowering Aathi tree at Ganapatiswaram. When he comes, tell him where we are." Saying this, the Lord went to the sacred Aathi tree and sat there, pleased. These are the verses. The Lord, in the form of Bhairava Jangama, came to Thiruchengattangudi and, like one with an insatiable hunger, asked everyone he met where Siruthondar's sacred house was, and quickly arrived there. Although He had no hunger, He acted as if He did. He inquired of everyone He met where Siruthondar's sacred house was and then proceeded to that house. Upon arriving, He asked, "Is Siruthondar, the devotee who always offers food to the servants of Lord Shiva, the one adorned with garlands of buzzing flowers, in this house?" asked the Lord who came in the guise of Bhairava Jangama. In Siruthonda Nayanar's sacred house, there was a maidservant named Sandanam. She was a great devotee of Shiva and His servants. It was the duty of the maidservants to greet and answer those who came from outside. The maidservant Sandanam, realizing that the one asking the question was a great ascetic, a devotee of Lord Shiva, came forward and bowed to the feet of the Lord in the Jangama form and answered the question. She bowed to the feet of that devotee and answered. This is the tradition of bowing to a devotee and answering. She addressed him as "Enthamai Aaludaiyavare" (Our Lord, who owns us) and answered. This shows her devotion to the devotees. The underlying meaning is that the one who came here is the Lord Himself, and He came to take possession of this entire family, including this maidservant, on that day. We see that the maidservant, unknowingly, spoke the words "Enthamai Aaludaiyavare." The maidservant Sandanam replied, "Siruthonda Nayanar has gone out to seek devotees of boundless glory. Our Lord, who owns us, please enter the house." Immediately, the Bhairava Jangama looked at the maidservant and said, "I will not enter alone where women are alone." This is a very special worldly principle, a great principle and a civilized behavior that people should follow. Therefore, the Lord states that He will not enter alone. He says this with the implication that He will come later with His divine consort, our Goddess, and our divine son. Hearing this, Thiruvengattu Ammai, Siruthonda Nayanar's wife, fearing that this devotee might leave, quickly rushed out of the house. On that day, no devotee had come to their house. Therefore, Siruthonda Nayanar had gone to seek a devotee. In this situation, Thiruvengattu Ammai, who was fully committed to her domestic duties and bore the entire burden of household responsibilities, rushed out, fearing that she might miss this devotee who had come on his own. Thiruvengattu Ammai said, "Siruthonda Nayanar, who has the vow of serving food to the devotees of Lord Ambalamudai, has gone searching today because he could not find any devotee to fulfill that vow. If he sees this unique and beautiful form you have assumed, he will be overjoyed, considering it a great blessing. He will not delay now. He will come immediately. Please remain here." Thus, Thiruvengattu Nangai Ammai appealed to the Bhairava Jangama devotee. "He will come very soon. Please remain here until he arrives." The Bhairava Jangama devotee replied, "We are from Uttarapathi, those who uphold unparalleled domestic virtue. We have come here to see Siruthondar, who is of incomparable glory. Therefore, we will not enter here if he is not present, under any circumstances." The Bhairava Jangama says, "We are from Uttarapathi." Uttarapathi means the northern country. This deity is still worshiped as Uttarapathiyar Uttarapatheeswarar in the Ganapatiswarar temple in Thiruchengattangudi. Therefore, He states that He will not enter under any circumstances if Siruthonda Nayanar is not present. He, who had concealed His third eye, said, "We are staying under the beautiful flowering Aathi tree at Ganapatiswaram. When he comes, tell him where we are." Saying this, the Lord went to the sacred Aathi tree and sat there, pleased. He says, "We are staying under the beautiful flowering Aathi tree at Ganapatiswaram." The word "irukkindrom" (we are staying) implies that "we will stay there forever." The sacred Aathi tree is still in the temple today. There is also a shrine for Uttarapathiyar under it. This sacred Aathi tree is a type of Aathi tree, called Kattaathi. Thus, the meaning of these six verses is established. Through these six verses and the previous verses, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman implicitly shows that the actions of Shiva Rupam and Shiva Darisanam, among the ten tasks bestowed by Lord Shiva, have occurred here. The 42nd verse: "Not finding any devotee anywhere, the virtuous Siruthondar returned to his prosperous home. His wife, filled with boundless joy, told him of the arrival of a devotee with a form cherished by the world." Siruthondar, who sought the devotees of Lord Shiva, who wears the Ganga, and could not find them anywhere, returned to his prosperous sacred house. He was the virtuous Siruthondar. The fact that he did not find any devotees until that day was due to his penance. It was due to his penance that the Lord Himself came to bestow grace upon him, without any other devotee coming. This was the great fruit of his penance. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman sings that he returned to his house without finding any devotee. Therefore, it is understood that until then, he was coming and going, searching for devotees, sometimes returning to his house and sometimes leaving. It was a prosperous sacred house, filled with Shiva's grace. It was a house filled with worldly wealth for Lord Shiva and His devotees, and also abundant spiritual wealth. The three instruments of mind, speech, and body, filled with grace, are indicated here. As soon as Siruthonda Nayanar arrived at his sacred house, his wife, Thiruvengattu Nangai Ammai, with great joy, told Siruthonda Nayanar the news of the arrival of the Bhairava Jangama, a joy that was immeasurable. "A devotee with a beautiful form, cherished by everyone who sees him, has arrived here." We saw that it was a form that would make the hearts and souls of those who contemplated it melt and overflow. We saw that a gentle smile, like moonlight, illuminated His gracious face. Accordingly, it was a beautiful form that everyone in the world who saw it would love. She tells him the news that a person with such a divine form had come here. The implication here is that the person with that divine form, who bore it for the world to see, was none other than Lord Shiva, whom the Vedas speak of as the one and only Lord. When Thiruvengattu Nangai Ammai said this, Siruthonda Nayanar, overjoyed, asked, "I am saved! Where is he now?" He said, "The one holding the sharp trident and the skull said, 'We are from the northern country.' He is a Bhairava Jangama holding a prosperous Damaru in one hand. Even though we asked and pleaded with him to stay inside the sacred house, he did not stay here and is now under the sacred Aathi tree in the Ganapatiswarar temple," said Thiruvengattu Nangai Ammai. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 07 — Siruthonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 930, authored by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarukkam. Siruthonda Nayanar Puranam. Since no Shiva devotees could be found for the Maheswara Puja, Siruthonda Nayanar returned home. His wife, Thiruvengattu Nangai Ammai, informed him about the arrival of a devotee in the guise of Bhairava. Hearing this, Siruthonda Nayanar was greatly pleased. Five continuous verses, 43, 44, 45, 46, 47, follow. They are as follows: "I am saved! Where is he?" Siruthondar asked. She replied, "The one with the sharp trident and skull said, 'We are from the northern country.' The Bhairava with the Damaru in his hand did not stay here, even though I asked him to, but went and stayed under the sacred Aathi tree at Ganapatiswaram." Hearing his wife's words, Siruthondar, with rising eagerness, quickly went, saw him, bowed at his sacred feet, and stood there. The devotee who stood there, the Lord, spoke, "Are you the great Siruthondar?" Siruthondar bowed to him and said, "Though I am unworthy to praise those adorned with sacred ash, the Lord's devotees, out of their compassion, will say that I am that person. I searched for faultless devotees in this ancient city with great love to feed them, but I could not find them. Now, by my penance, I have found you. Please come to my house and partake of food." The devotee, whom even the great Vishnu does not know, said, "O ascetic, we have come to see you. We are from Uttarapathi. It is difficult for you to feed us with love. It is not possible for you to do so." Siruthondar replied, "I will not speak without thought. O ascetic, whose form is filled with grace, please tell me the nature of the food you partake of, so I can quickly prepare it. If the Lord's devotees are found, even what is deemed impossible can be achieved. There is no difficulty." These are the verses. As soon as he heard what his wife said, Siruthonda Nayanar asked, "I am saved! Tell me where he is." He says, "The daily vow I observe will not be broken today, but will continue. Therefore, I am saved." Thiruvengattu Nangai Ammai replied, "The Bhairava devotee who came was holding a sharp trident and a skull. He said he was from the northern country. He is a Bhairava Jangama holding a prosperous Damaru in his hand. Even though we asked and pleaded with him to stay inside the sacred house, he did not stay here and went and is now under the sacred Aathi tree in the Ganapatiswarar temple," said Thiruvengattu Nangai Ammai. Hearing his wife's words, Siruthondar, with increasing eagerness, quickly went and saw the Bhairava Jangama, bowed at his sacred feet, and stood there. This shows the great eagerness our Nayanar had to see the devotee. When one goes to visit great people, one should stand silently before them, awaiting their words, and convey one's purpose implicitly. One should answer only what they ask, and only to that extent. These are the traditions. Here too, the Nayanar quickly went, fell at the lotus feet of the devotee, bowed, and stood respectfully. The Bhairava devotee, seeing him, asked, "Are you the great Siruthondar?" The devotee asked, "Are you the one called the great Siruthondar?" The divine Sekkizhar Peruman, as a divine poet, uses the contradictory terms "Periya" (great) and "Siru" (small) with poetic beauty. "Periya Siruthondar" means the great Siruthondar. He is great due to his fame and his devotion to Lord Shiva and His devotees. Furthermore, he is well-known by the name Siruthondar, rather than his birth name Paranjothiyar, which is a descriptive name. We also see this tradition here. When asked this, Siruthonda Nayanar bowed to the Lord, who was the devotee, and said, "I am unworthy to go before the devotees adorned with sacred ash and praise them. Nevertheless, the devotees of Shiva, out of their compassion for me, will say that I am that person." "Poothi Ani Sadhanathavargal" means Shiva devotees adorned with sacred ash. "Sadhanam" here refers to Shiva Sadhanam, which are Vibhuti (sacred ash) and Rudraksha. Therefore, he says, "Even to stand before such Shiva devotees and praise them, I am unworthy. However, Shiva devotees are compassionate and have the grace to uplift souls, making even the unworthy worthy. They are capable of destroying the three impurities, which are the faults and evils of living beings. To feed such devotees, I searched everywhere in this ancient city with great desire, but I could not find them until now. Now, by my penance, I have found you. I found you by the fruit of my past penance," praised Siruthonda Nayanar. This is true. If the Lord Himself comes from Mount Kailash, the sacred mountain, in the form of a Bhairava, to experience and bless Siruthonda Nayanar's devotion, as a result of his accumulated penance, then to see Him, one needs immense penance in this world. It is rare, rare, rare to find someone who performed great penance and received great grace like Siruthonda Nayanar. Therefore, what the Nayanar said, "I found you by penance," is absolutely true. Siruthonda Nayanar further appealed to the Bhairava devotee. He requested, "Please come to my house and partake of sacred food." The devotee, whom even the great Vishnu does not know, replied to Siruthonda Nayanar, "O ascetic, we have come to see you. We are from the northern country. It is difficult for you to feed us with love. That act is very rare. Therefore, you cannot do it." He says, "Not only for you, but for anyone else, this act is rare." The Bhairava Jangama who had come here confirmed it three times, saying, "You cannot feed us with love. That act is rare. It is not possible." Our Siruthonda Nayanar replied and appealed. "Swami, I will not say anything without careful consideration. O ascetic, whose form is filled with grace, please tell me the nature of the food you partake of. I need to prepare it quickly. I need to prepare the sacred food suitable for you. Therefore, please tell me how you partake of sacred food. You say that it is impossible for me to feed you sacred food. If the Lord's devotees are found, even what is deemed impossible can be achieved. Therefore, this task is not difficult for me," said Siruthonda Nayanar. "Even what you said is impossible will happen. It will happen by the grace of the Lord's devotees." Siruthonda Nayanar says this, not out of self-assertion, but because of the greatness of the devotees. The Uttarapathiyar, who had said "impossible, rare, not possible" three times, was refuted by Siruthonda Nayanar, saying that it would happen by the grace, compassion, and greatness of the devotees. We must understand this tradition and subtlety. The tradition here is that one should not immediately refute what elders say, but rather agree with it and then appeal with a refutation. Siruthonda Nayanar, addressing the devotee, says, "Even though what you said, that it is impossible, is true, it is possible by the grace of the devotee." He agrees with the Bhairava devotee's statement and then appeals with a counter-argument. The 48th verse: When Siruthondar said, "There is nothing difficult," the great Lord in the beautiful Bhairava form replied, "O devotee, who loves us so much, we partake of a human sacrifice after three seasons. Today is the day for that. It will be difficult for you to feed us." When Siruthondar said, "There is nothing difficult," the Lord, who had assumed the beautiful Bhairava form, replied, "O Siruthondar, who has great love for feeding Shiva devotees and great affection for feeding us, we have a vow to partake of a human sacrifice only once after three seasons. Today is the day for that. However, it will be very difficult for you to feed us in that manner." "Mooviruthu" means six months. "Ruthu" is a period of two months. Three Ruthus mean six months. Therefore, the Bhairava Jangama states that it is his vow to partake of a human sacrifice once every six months, and today is that day. "Pasu Veezhthida Unpathu" means to sacrifice a human and partake of it as food. He says, "We will sacrifice a human and partake of it as food. That is our vow." "Pasu" here refers to the food partaken by the Bhairava sect. Here, "Pasu" does not mean a cow; it generally refers to an animal. Bhairavaism is one of the six outer-inner sects. It is mostly associated with Vamamarga. However, it differs slightly from Vamamarga in its practices. Bhairavaism is a sect that believes Bhairava is the supreme being and that joining the Bhairava sect leads to liberation. Some followers of this sect partake of meat. Just as some Vamamargis consume alcohol, some followers of the Bhairava sect also consume meat. Here, "Pasu" refers to meat. It is inappropriate to say "cow's meat," because cow's meat is not acceptable to any religion. Therefore, we must clearly remember that "Pasu" here generally refers only to meat. Thus, the Bhairava Jangama states. Our Siruthonda Nayanar also replies and appeals to him. We will continue to contemplate that. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 08 — Siruthonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 931, authored by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Arakonda Vanamulaiyal Sarukkam. Siruthonda Nayanar Puranam. The Bhairava devotee reveals his method of eating to Siruthonda Nayanar. The 49th verse: "It is very good, I have plenty. I have no lack here. If you, the devotee of the one who swallowed poison, would kindly tell me what kind of 'pasu' is suitable for your food, I will quickly prepare the food and return without delay," he said, bowing his hands. Siruthonda Nayanar, having heard the words of the Bhairava devotee, replied and appealed. "It is very good, I have three kinds of herds. I have no lack here," he said. The Bhairava devotee had said that he partakes of a "pasu" that is sacrificed, meaning he eats meat. So, Siruthonda Nayanar replies to this. "I have three kinds of herds, three groups." Therefore, he rejoices, saying, "It is very good." The three kinds of herds are cattle, sheep, and buffalo. "I have all three kinds. Therefore, there is no lack. It is very good," he rejoiced. "If you, the devotee of Lord Shiva who swallowed poison, would kindly tell me what kind of 'pasu' is suitable for your food, I will quickly prepare the food accordingly and return without missing the appointed time," he said, bowing his hands. The Lord Himself swallowed poison. Therefore, you are a devotee of such a Lord. So, there is no fault in partaking of a "pasu" as great food, Siruthonda Nayanar implicitly appealed. He requested to be informed of what it is. He appealed, "Please tell me which type of animal is suitable for your sacred food." The Lord's coming here and asking for meat is not about the nature of non-vegetarian food or beef. The purpose and philosophical meaning of this is different. Its aim is to show that the deep attachment people have to their children, their offspring, will not stand before the great love of Shiva's devotees, which is the state of Siruthonda Nayanar's boundless love. In this world, the most difficult and inconsolable sorrow is said to be the sorrow of losing a child. People in the world are deeply attached to their children. But this attachment will not stand before the service of Shiva's devotees. Such great lovers are Shiva's devotees. The Lord performs this divine act here to show this state to the world. He had said that he partakes of food once every six months, and today is that day. Accordingly, Siruthonda Nayanar asks, "If you tell me which animal is suitable for your sacred food, I will prepare it as food accordingly and immediately serve it to you without delay." He appeals. The 50th verse: Seeing the great love of the virtuous Siruthondar, the Bhairava replied, "O beloved one, the 'pasu' we sacrifice for our food is a human 'pasu.' We partake of a human 'pasu' that is five years old and without any blemish on its limbs. Furthermore, if I tell you, it will be like piercing a wound with a spear." Siruthonda Nayanar, who appealed, "We have three kinds of herds. Please tell me what kind of 'pasu,' what kind of food, you will partake of," seeing the great love of Siruthondar, who was deeply devoted to Lord Shiva and His devotees, the Lord, who came as Bhairava Jangama, spoke with grace. "O Siruthondar, who has great love for devotees, the 'pasu' we sacrifice for our food is a human 'pasu.' It is a human 'pasu' that is five years old and without any blemish on its limbs," he said with grace. He states, "The 'pasu' suitable for our food, for us to partake of, is a human 'pasu' among humans." And what are the characteristics of that human 'pasu'? He says, "It must be five years old. And it must be without any defects or flaws in its limbs. There should be no external marks or scars. Similarly, there should be no defects in the inner flesh." And then he adds, "There is one more thing. If I tell you that, it will be like piercing a wound with a spear." Having a wound is one kind of suffering. Piercing it with a spear is another kind of suffering. The human 'pasu,' five years old, with unblemished limbs, are like painful wounds. The characteristic he is about to mention will be like piercing this wound with a spear, says the Bhairava devotee. That human 'pasu,' five years old and without blemish, how should it be? How should it be prepared as food? He is about to say that it should be the good son of a family. The mother should hold him, and the father should, with great joy, knowingly prepare him. Saying this is compared to piercing a wound with a spear. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman's simile here was later used by Kamban in his Kamba Ramayanam. The words spoken by the great ascetic Viswamitra to Dasaratha, "Give me one of your four dark-skinned sons," were like "a spear piercing a fresh wound, or fire entering a deep chasm," as Kamban describes in the Kamba Ramayanam, renewing and cherishing this simile. There too, we see the same sorrow of losing a child. And there too, we see that Dasaratha went to heaven because of that sorrow of losing a child. The 51st verse: "There is nothing difficult now. Please tell me," said Siruthondar. The Lord replied, "We partake of the curry prepared without fault, with the father and mother joyfully agreeing, when the mother holds the good son, the only son of a family, and the father knows about it." This is the 51st verse. When the Bhairava devotee asked, "There is one more thing," Siruthonda Nayanar appealed, "There is nothing difficult for me. Please tell me quickly." He says, "Please tell me quickly without any further delay." Generally, the Bhairava devotee, as if hesitant to speak such a cruel message that goes against worldly norms, gradually adds more and more conditions. Therefore, Siruthonda Nayanar appealed, "Do not be afraid, tell me quickly." The Lord hesitated to speak in such a way, as if to say that one needs courage to undertake such a great act in a worldly sense, and He spoke in a way that those who listen would gradually understand. But our Siruthonda Nayanar, being full of great love for feeding devotees, expressed his eagerness to feed the devotee by saying, "Do not tell me one by one, but tell me quickly what else needs to be done." When Siruthonda Nayanar appealed thus, the Lord also said, "That human 'pasu' must be a good son, a male child, the only son of a family. The mother should hold him, and the father should know about it. And at that time, the father, mother, and son, all three, should be joyful and without fault. The curry prepared without fault is the food we partake of," he said with grace. The 52nd verse: Hearing the Lord's words, the devotee replied, "Even this is not difficult for me, if my Lord will partake of it." With the joy of having received his quick consent, he bowed at his lotus feet, fragrant and soft, and returned to his house. Siruthonda Nayanar, having heard all that the Lord said, said, "O my Lord, if you will partake of it, even this is not a difficult task for me. It is not a hard task." He quickly took leave of the Bhairava devotee and, with great joy and love, bowed at the soft, lotus-like feet of the Bhairava devotee, which were filled with honey, and returned to his sacred house. Siruthonda Nayanar was determined to make his son the food. Therefore, he firmly told the Bhairava devotee that it was not difficult. Having said that, the Bhairava devotee's sacred feet were like freshly bloomed lotuses, beautiful, red, soft, sweet, fragrant, and all-giving, bestowing the honey of Shiva's bliss. Siruthonda Nayanar bowed to those sacred feet and returned to his sacred house. The 53rd and 54th verses: The wife of great chastity, Thiruvengattu Ammai, came forward, looking for Siruthondar's arrival. Seeing his face blooming with increasing joy, she bowed at his feet. Then, looking at her husband's face, she inquired about the actions of the great ascetic. The generous one, looking at his wife, said, "The great ascetic who came agreed to partake of food with a joyful heart, if a good son, the only son of a family, five years old, without any blemish on his limbs, is held by his mother, and his father, with joy, knowingly prepares him." Thiruvengattu Ammai, a divine woman of great chastity and love, stood at the front door of her mansion, awaiting Siruthondar's arrival. As she waited, Siruthondar arrived, and she saw the radiant joy on his face. She saw Siruthondar's face blooming. Seeing her husband's face blooming, she bowed at his feet and then, looking at her husband's face, inquired about the actions of the Bhairava devotee, the ascetic. This indicates and shows the Ammai's eagerness and enthusiasm to feed the devotees. We see that Thiruvengattu Ammai was waiting at the doorstep, so that there would be no delay if her husband, who had gone to meet the Bhairava devotee, came inside the house to tell her the news. She was so eager that she did not want even that delay to happen. Just as it is said, "The beauty of the inner self will be seen on the face," seeing her husband's blooming face, she rejoiced, realizing that he must have certainly obtained consent to serve food to the devotee today. As stated in the Thirukkural, "a mind like a clear crystal," she asked what the Bhairava devotee had said. Then, Siruthonda Nayanar, the generous one, looking at his wife, Thiruvengattu Ammai, said, "The great ascetic who came agreed to partake of sacred food with a joyful heart, if a good son, the only son of a family, five years old, without any blemish on his limbs, is held by his mother, and his father, with joy, knowingly prepares him." These are the words Siruthonda Nayanar spoke to his wife. The 55th and 56th verses: The wife of rare chastity, looking at him, said, "We will prepare the food in the proper way here for the great Bhairava devotee to partake of. How can we obtain such a child, the only son of a family?" As she bowed, her husband, looking at her face, said, "Are there any who would give their sons for ample wealth? No father or mother would knowingly give their son. Without delay, let us call our son, whom you bore to save me here." As soon as Siruthonda Nayanar gave that answer, his wife, a woman of rare chastity, looked at her husband, Siruthonda Nayanar, and said, "To prepare food for the great Bhairava devotee to partake of, how can we obtain such a child, the only son of a family?"
